\displaystyle\;\alpha_{s}\big)ds\bigg]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{t+\delta}L\left(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},0\right)ds+\int_{t+\delta}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle=\delta\mathbb{E}[L(X_{t}/\varepsilon,0)]+\frac{T-t-\delta}{T-t}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}^{n}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}^{n}\frac{T-t}{T-t-\delta}\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle\leq\delta K_{0}+\frac{T-t-\delta}{T-t}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}^{n}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}^{n}\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{T-t-\delta}{T-t}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\left(L\left(\frac{X_{s}^{n}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}^{n}\frac{T-t}{T-t-\delta}\right)-L\left(\frac{X_{s}^{n}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}^{n}\right)\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle\leq\delta K_{0}+\frac{T-t-\delta}{T-t}(U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)+1/n)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{T-t-\delta}{T-t}\cdot\frac{\delta C_{0}}{T-t-\delta}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\left(1+\frac{\delta}{T-t-\delta}|\alpha_{s}^{n}|\right)|\alpha_{s}^{n}|ds\right]

\displaystyle\leq\delta\left(K_{0}-\frac{U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)}{T-t}\right)+\frac{1}{n}+U_{ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)+\frac{\delta C}{T-t}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}(|\alpha_{s}^{n}|^{2}+1)ds\right]

\displaystyle\leq\delta\left(K_{0}-\frac{U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)}{T-t}\right)+\frac{1}{n}+U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)+\frac{C\delta}{T-t}\left(T-t+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}|\alpha_{s}^{n}|^{2}ds\right]\right)

\displaystyle\leq U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)+\delta\left(K_{0}-\frac{U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)}{T-t}\right)+\frac{1}{n}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{C\delta}{T-t}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}^{n}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}^{n}\right)ds\right]+K_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\leq U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)+\frac{1}{n}+\delta C\left(1+\frac{1}{n(T-t)}+\frac{U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)}{T-t}\right)

where in the last inequality we used Lemma
7.1
. For
n
large and
\delta
small enough, we see that the last two terms on the right hand side become very small. This implies that for
\varepsilon_{0}>0
, we can choose
n,\delta
such that

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t}^{T}L\big(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},\;\alpha_{s}\big)ds\bigg]\leq U^{\varepsilon}_{i,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)+\varepsilon_{0}.

The reverse inequality follows.

To prove the second statement, we observe that the above chain of inequalities implies

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t}^{T}L\big(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},\;\alpha_{s}\big)ds\bigg]\leq U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)+\frac{1}{n}+\delta C\left(1+\frac{1}{n(T-t)}+\frac{U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)}{T-t}\right).

Choosing
n
to be large enough so that
\frac{1}{n}<\delta
and since
\frac{1}{n}\leq 1
we derive

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t}^{T}L\big(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},\;\alpha_{s}\big)ds\bigg]\leq U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)+\delta+\delta C\left(1+\frac{1}{(T-t)}+\frac{|U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)|}{T-t}\right)\leq U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)+\delta C_{0},

where
C_{0}:=1+C\left(1+\frac{1}{(T-t)}+\frac{|\sup_{m\in\mathcal{P}_{2}:{\bf d}_{2}^{2}(\delta_{0},m)\leq R}U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,m,\nu)|}{T-t}\right)
. Here, we used the fact that
\mu
satisfies
{\bf d}_{2}^{2}(\delta_{0},\mu)\leq\int|x|^{2}\mu(dx)\leq R
.

We let
\eta>0
so that
\eta C_{0}^{-1}\leq T-t
. Choosing
\delta
such that
\delta C_{0}=\eta
, the above inequality gives

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{t}^{T}L\big(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},\;\alpha_{s}\big)ds\bigg]\leq U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)+\eta.

This means that
(\alpha_{s})_{s\in[t,T]}
is an
\eta
-optimal control for
U^{\varepsilon}_{i,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)
and, since it is also in
\mathcal{A}_{t,\mu,\nu}^{i,\delta}
, the equality
\delta=C_{0}^{-1}\eta
implies
(\alpha_{s})_{s\in[t,T]}\in\mathcal{A}_{t,\mu,\nu}^{i,C_{0}^{-1}\eta}
. So the constant
C=C_{t,\nu,R}
in the second statement is
C_{0}^{-1}
from above.
∎

We now study the dependence of
U_{ot}^{\varepsilon}
with respect to the initial time variable
t
.

Theorem 7.4
.

Fix
\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\delta\in(0,T)
and
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Then there exists a constant
C=C(\mu,\nu,\delta)>0
such that

|U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)-U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t^{\prime},\mu,\nu)|\;\leq\;C\,|t-t^{\prime}|

for all
t,t^{\prime}\in[0,T-\delta]
.

Proof.

We split the proof into two steps. Assume
t<t^{\prime}

Step 1: Upper bound.
Since
\mu,\nu
are fixed, we let
C=C_{t,\nu,R}
(for
R>\int|x|^{2}\mu(dx)
) be the constant given by Proposition
7.3
.
We also consider
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}_{t,\mu,\nu}^{i,t^{\prime}-t}
a
\frac{t^{\prime}-t}{C}
-optimal control and
(X_{s})_{s\in[t,T]}
the associated state process. By definition the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{t,\mu,\nu}^{i,t^{\prime}-t}
, it is true that
\alpha|_{s\in[t^{\prime},T]}\in\mathcal{A}_{t^{\prime},\mu,\nu}^{i}
, therefore we compute

\displaystyle U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t^{\prime},\mu,\nu)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t^{\prime}}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}\right)ds\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}\right)ds\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{t^{\prime}}L\left(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},0\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle\leq U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)+\frac{t^{\prime}-t}{C}+K_{0}(t^{\prime}-t)=U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)+(t^{\prime}-t)(K_{0}+1/C).

Step 2: Lower bound.

Let
\alpha^{\prime}
be an admissible control for
U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t^{\prime},\mu,\nu)
.
We extend it to
[t,T]
by setting
\alpha^{\prime}_{s}:=0
for
s\in[t,t^{\prime})
and

X_{s}:=X_{t^{\prime}}
on
[t,t^{\prime}]
.
Then
X_{t}\sim\mu
and
X_{T}\sim\nu
.

Using (
2.8
), we obtain

\displaystyle U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)\leq\mathbb{E}\int_{t}^{t^{\prime}}L\Big(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},0\Big)\,ds+\mathbb{E}\int_{t^{\prime}}^{T}L\Big(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},\alpha^{\prime}_{s}\Big)\,ds\leq K_{0}(t^{\prime}-t)+U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t^{\prime},\mu,\nu).

Combining the two estimates yields the desired Lipschitz continuity.
∎

Proposition 7.5
.

Fix
t<T
,
\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
i\in\{1,2\}
. Then the map
\mu\mapsto U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)
is continuous on
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with respect to the
{\bf d}_{2}
-distance.
Moreover, for every
R>0
there exists a constant
\overline{C}=\overline{C}(R,T-t,C_{t,\nu,R})>0,
where
C_{t,\nu,R}
is the constant constructed in Proposition
7.3
, such that

|U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu_{1},\nu)-U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu_{2},\nu)|\leq\overline{C}\,{\bf d}_{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}),

for all
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

with
\int|x|^{2}\,d\mu_{i}\leq R,\;\;i=1,2
.

Proof.

We fix
R>0
and
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x|^{2}\;\mu_{i}(dx)\leq R,\;i=1,2
.

Let
\delta\in(0,T-t)
and
\alpha^{\delta}\in\mathcal{A}_{t,\mu_{1},\nu}^{i,\delta}
a
C^{-1}\delta
-optimal control for
U^{\varepsilon}_{i,ot}(t,\mu_{1},\nu)
provided by Proposition
7.3
, where the constant
C=C_{t,\nu,R}
was also constructed in Proposition
7.3
. We denote by
(X_{s}^{\delta})_{s\in[t,T]}
the associated state process. By the
\delta
-optimality and the quadratic growth of
L
, we have

(7.5)

\frac{1}{4C_{2,H}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t+\delta}^{T}|\alpha_{s}^{\delta}|^{2}ds\right]-K_{0}(T-t)\leq U^{\varepsilon}_{i,ot}(t,\mu_{1},\nu)+C^{-1}\delta\leq\frac{1}{2C_{1,H}(T-t)}{\bf d}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{1},\nu)+K_{0}(T-t)+C^{-1}\delta.

We consider
Z\sim N(0,I_{d})
to be a standard normal
d
-dimensional random variable which is independent of
X_{t+\delta}^{\delta}
. Then, for
r>0
,
X_{t+\delta}^{\delta}+rZ
has absolutely continuous distribution with respect to the Lebesgue measure; we denote
\mathcal{L}(X_{t+\delta}^{\delta}+rZ)=\mu_{1}^{r}
. Since
\mu_{1}^{r}
is absolutely continuous, we can also pick
(\alpha_{s}^{ot})_{s\in[t,t+\delta]}
an optimal control for the optimal transport problem from
\mu_{2}
to
\mu_{1}^{r}
with quadratic cost
L(x,v)=|v|^{2}
such that the associated state process
(X_{s}^{ot})_{s\in[t,t+\delta]}
satisfies
X_{t+\delta}^{ot}=X_{t+\delta}^{\delta}+rZ\sim\mu_{1}^{r}
. In particular, we may take

X_{s}^{ot}=\left(1-\frac{s-t}{\delta}\right)T(X_{t+\delta}^{\delta}+rZ)+\frac{s-t}{\delta}(X_{t+\delta}^{\delta}+rZ),

where
T:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is the Brenier map from
\mu_{1}^{r}
to
\mu_{2}
. We also consider the control
\alpha
and the associated state process as follows

\alpha_{s}=\begin{cases}\alpha_{s}^{\delta}-\frac{rZ}{T-t-\delta},&\text{if }s\in[t+\delta,T],\\
\alpha_{s}^{ot},&\text{if }s\in[t,t+\delta)\end{cases},\quad\text{and}\quad X_{s}=X_{t}^{ot}+\int_{t}^{s}\alpha_{\tau}d\tau,\;\;s\in[t,T].

It is clear that
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}_{t,\mu_{2},\nu}^{i}
, because
X_{t}=X_{t}^{ot}=T(X_{t+\delta}^{\delta}+rZ)\sim\mu_{2}
and
X_{T}=X_{T}^{\delta}\sim\nu
. We have

\displaystyle U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,

\displaystyle\mu_{2},\nu)\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}\right)ds\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{t+\delta}L\left(\frac{X_{s}^{ot}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}^{ot}\right)ds+\int_{t+\delta}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle\leq\delta K_{0}+\frac{{\bf d}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{1}^{r},\mu_{2})}{2C_{1,H}\delta}+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t+\delta}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}^{\delta}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}^{\delta}\right)ds\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\left|L\left(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}\right)-L\left(\frac{X_{s}^{\delta}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}^{\delta}\right)\right|ds\right]

\displaystyle\leq\delta K_{0}+\frac{{\bf d}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{1}^{r},\mu_{2})}{2C_{1,H}\delta}+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}^{\delta}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}^{\delta}\right)ds\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{t+\delta}_{t}L\left(\frac{X_{s}^{\delta}}{\varepsilon},\alpha_{s}^{\delta}\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+C_{L}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t+\delta}^{T}(1+|\alpha_{s}|+|\alpha_{s}^{\delta}|)\left(\left|\frac{X_{s}-X_{s}^{\delta}}{\varepsilon}\right|+|\alpha_{s}-\alpha_{s}^{\delta}|\right)ds\right]

\displaystyle\leq U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu_{1},\nu)+C^{-1}\delta+\frac{{\bf d}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{1}^{r},\mu_{2})}{2C_{1,H}\delta}+2\delta K_{0}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+C_{L}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t+\delta}^{T}(1+|\alpha_{s}|+|\alpha_{s}^{\delta}|)\left(\left|\frac{X_{s}-X_{s}^{\delta}}{\varepsilon}\right|+|\alpha_{s}-\alpha_{s}^{\delta}|\right)ds\right],

where we repeatedly used the regularity and growth properties of
L.
However, for
s\in[t+\delta,T]
,

X_{s}-X_{s}^{\delta}=rZ-rZ\frac{s-t-\delta}{T-t-\delta}\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha_{s}-\alpha_{s}^{\delta}=\frac{rZ}{T-t-\delta},

hence the last term in the above inequality can be bounded by

\displaystyle C_{L}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t+\delta}^{T}\left(1+|\alpha_{s}^{\delta}|+\frac{r|Z|}{T-t-\delta}\right)\left(\frac{r|Z|}{\varepsilon}\frac{T-s}{T-t-\delta}+\frac{r|Z|}{T-t-\delta}\right)ds\right]\leq rC^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+1\right),

for some constant
C^{\prime}
depending only on
R,\nu
and
T-t
, where in the last step we also used (
7.5
) and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Plugging this back in the above yields

U^{\varepsilon}_{i,ot}(t,\mu_{2},\nu)\leq U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu_{1},\nu)+C^{-1}\delta+\frac{{\bf d}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{1}^{r},\mu_{2})}{2C_{1,H}\delta}+2\delta K_{0}+rC^{\prime}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}+1\right).

For
r\to 0^{+}
, this implies

U^{\varepsilon}_{i,ot}(t,\mu_{2},\nu)\leq U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu_{1},\nu)+C^{-1}\delta+\frac{{\bf d}_{2}^{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}{2C_{1,H}\delta}+2\delta K_{0}.

Now we choose
\delta=\frac{T-t}{4R^{2}}{\bf d}_{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
. We observe
\delta<T-t
, because
{\bf d}_{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})<4R^{2}
, and that this choice in the above inequality gives

U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu_{2},\nu)\leq U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu_{1},\nu)+\overline{C}{\bf d}_{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}),

for some constant
\overline{C}>0
depending only on
R,\;C\text{ and }T-t
. We may similarly prove the inequality
U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu_{1},\nu)\leq U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu_{2},\nu)+\overline{C}{\bf d}_{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}),
and this finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 7.6
.

Fix
t<T
,
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Then the map
\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\ni\nu\mapsto U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu)
is continuous on

\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with respect to the
{\bf d}_{2}
-distance. Moreover, for every
R>0
there exists
C=C(R,T-t)>0
such that

|U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu_{1})-U_{i,ot}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu,\nu_{2})|\leq C\,{\bf d}_{2}(\nu_{1},\nu_{2}),

for all
\nu_{1},\nu_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

with
\int|x|^{2}\,d\nu_{i}\leq R,\;i=1,2
.

Proof.

The proof is identical to Proposition
7.5
after reversing the time.
∎

7.2.
Convergence of
U_{ot}^{\varepsilon}

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

Step 1.
(
U^{\varepsilon}_{ot}=U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}
and
U_{ot}=U_{1,ot}
)

We first notice that Proposition
4.7
implies

U_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu)=\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}}\left\{U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)+F(\nu)\right\}=\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}}\left\{U^{\varepsilon}_{2,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)+F(\nu)\right\}=U_{2}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mu),

for any
F\colon\mathcal{P}_{2}\to\mathbb{R}
bounded and Lipschitz and any
(t,\mu)\in[0,T)\times\mathcal{P}_{2}
.
Since
U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot},U^{\varepsilon}_{2,ot}
are lower bounded (Lemma
7.1
) and the maps
\nu\mapsto U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu),\;\nu\mapsto U^{\varepsilon}_{2,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)
are continuous (Proposition
7.6
), it follows from Lemma
7.2
that
U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}=U^{\varepsilon}_{2,ot}
. This equality yields
U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}=U^{\varepsilon}_{ot}=U^{\varepsilon}_{2,ot}
, because of (
7.1
).

By the regularity and growth properties of the effective cost
\overline{L}
in Proposition
2.1
, we may argue similarly for the value function
U_{ot}
from (
1.15
) to derive

U_{ot}(t,\mu,\nu)=U_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu):=\inf_{\alpha\in\mathcal{A}^{1}_{t,\mu,\nu}}\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\overline{L}(X_{s},\alpha_{s},\mathcal{L}(X_{s}))ds\right]\right\},

for any
(t,\mu,\nu)\in[0,T)\times\mathcal{P}_{2}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}
.

Step 2.
(Convergence and rate of convergence under (A1))

Fix
(t,\mu,\nu)\in[0,T)\times\mathcal{P}_{2}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}
and set
M_{0}:={\bf d}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\nu)
. Using the notation introduced in subsection
4.2
, we write

U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)=\inf_{(X,Y)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\nu}}\left\{\inf_{\gamma\in\Gamma(X,Y)}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{\gamma_{s}}{\varepsilon},\gamma_{s},\dot{\gamma}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{s})\right)\,ds\right]\right\}.

We argue as in the proof of Theorem
1.2
. Let
(X,Y,\gamma)
be
\delta
-optimal for
U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)
. Then

(7.6)

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{\gamma_{s}}{\varepsilon},\gamma_{s},\dot{\gamma}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{s})\right)\,ds\right]\leq U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)+\delta.

By Lemma
7.1
and the growth properties of
L
, we discover that there exists a constant
C=C(d,C_{1,H},C_{2,H},C_{G},K_{0})>0
such that

(7.7)

\frac{1}{T-t}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}|\dot{\gamma}_{s}|^{2}ds\right]\leq C\left(1+\frac{M_{0}}{(T-t)^{2}}\right).

Consider a time partition
t_{k}:=t+\frac{k}{N}(T-t)
for
k=0,\dots,N
, where
N\in\mathbb{N}
will be chosen later. With
J_{\varepsilon}
as in Lemma
4.6
, we may write

(7.8)

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{\gamma_{s}}{\varepsilon},\gamma_{s},\dot{\gamma}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{s})\right)\,ds\right]\geq J_{\varepsilon}(\gamma)-\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}E_{k},

where

E_{k}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\left|L\left(\frac{\gamma_{s}}{\varepsilon},\gamma_{s},\dot{\gamma}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{s})\right)-L\left(\frac{\gamma_{s}}{\varepsilon},\gamma_{t_{k}},\dot{\gamma}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{t_{k}})\right)\right|\,ds\right].

By Lemma
4.6
,

(7.9)

\displaystyle J_{\varepsilon}(\gamma)\geq\inf_{\eta\in\Gamma(X,Y;\gamma)}J_{\varepsilon}(\eta)=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\left[h^{\varepsilon}\left(\gamma_{t_{k}},\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{t_{k}});t_{k},t_{k+1},\gamma_{t_{k}},\gamma_{t_{k+1}}\right)\right].

Next, Proposition
6.2
implies that

(7.10)

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}&\left[h^{\varepsilon}\left(\gamma_{t_{k}},\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{t_{k}});t_{k},t_{k+1},\gamma_{t_{k}},\gamma_{t_{k+1}}\right)\right]\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{h}\left(\gamma_{t_{k}},\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{t_{k}});t_{k},t_{k+1},\gamma_{t_{k}},\gamma_{t_{k+1}}\right)\right]-C\left(1+\frac{\mathbb{E}\left|\gamma_{k+1}-\gamma_{k}\right|^{2}}{|t_{k+1}-t_{k}|^{2}}\right)\varepsilon,\end{split}

for some constant
C=C(d,C_{1,H},C_{2,H},K_{0})>0
. By Hölder’s inequality,

\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left|\gamma_{k+1}-\gamma_{k}\right|^{2}}{|t_{k+1}-t_{k}|^{2}}\leq\frac{N}{T-t}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\left|\dot{\gamma}_{s}\right|^{2}\right]\,ds\leq NC\left(1+\frac{M_{0}}{(T-t)^{2}}\right),

where we use the fact that
t_{k+1}-t_{k}=\frac{T-t}{N}
and (
7.7
). Combining this with (
7.10
) yields

(7.11)

\begin{split}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}&\left[h^{\varepsilon}\left(\gamma_{t_{k}},\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{t_{k}});t_{k},t_{k+1},\gamma_{t_{k}},\gamma_{t_{k+1}}\right)\right]\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\geq\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{h}\left(\gamma_{t_{k}},\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{t_{k}});t_{k},t_{k+1},\gamma_{t_{k}},\gamma_{t_{k+1}}\right)\right]-CN\varepsilon\left(1+\frac{M_{0}}{(T-t)^{2}}\right).\end{split}

Let
\overline{\gamma}:[t_{0},T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be the piecewise linear interpolation of
\gamma
on the partition
\{t_{k}\}_{k=0}^{N}
, namely

\overline{\gamma}(t,\omega):=\gamma(t_{k},\omega)+\frac{s-t_{k}}{t_{k+1}-t_{k}}\left(\gamma(t_{k+1},\omega)-\gamma(t_{k},\omega)\right),\qquad\text{ for }t\in\left[t_{k},t_{k+1}\right].

By Lemma
6.3
(for
\left(1+\frac{M_{0}}{(T-t)^{2}}\right)
in place of
M_{1}
),

(7.12)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\overline{h}\left(\gamma_{t_{k}},\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{t_{k}});t_{k},t_{k+1},\gamma_{t_{k}},\gamma_{t_{k+1}}\right)\right]=\inf_{\eta\in\Gamma(X,Y;\gamma)}\overline{J}(\eta)=\overline{J}(\overline{\gamma})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\overline{L}\left(\overline{\gamma}_{s},\dot{\overline{\gamma}}_{s},\mathcal{L}\left(\overline{\gamma}_{s}\right)\right)\,ds\right]-\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\overline{E}_{k},

where

\overline{E}_{k}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\left|\overline{L}\left(\gamma_{t_{k}},\dot{\overline{\gamma}}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{t_{k}})\right)-\overline{L}\left(\overline{\gamma}_{s},\dot{\overline{\gamma}}_{s},\mathcal{L}\left(\overline{\gamma}_{s}\right)\right)\right|\,ds\right].

Combining (
7.8
), (
7.9
), (
7.11
), and (
7.12
), we obtain

(7.13)

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{\gamma_{s}}{\varepsilon},\gamma_{s},\dot{\gamma}_{s},\mathcal{L}(\gamma_{s})\right)\,ds\right]\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\overline{L}\left(\overline{\gamma}_{s},\dot{\overline{\gamma}}_{s},\mathcal{L}\left(\overline{\gamma}_{s}\right)\right)\,ds\right]-\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}E_{k}-\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\overline{E}_{k}-C_{0}N\varepsilon,

where
C_{0}:=C\left(1+\frac{M_{0}}{(T-t)^{2}}\right)
. By Lemmas
4.6
,
6.3
(for
M=M_{1}=1+\frac{M_{0}}{(T-t)^{2}}=C_{0}/C
), and (
7.7
),

(7.14)

\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}E_{k}\leq\frac{C_{2}(1+C_{0}/C)(T-t)^{2}}{N},\qquad\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\overline{E}_{k}\leq\frac{C_{2}(1+C_{0}/C)(T-t)^{2}}{N},

for some constant
C_{2}=C(d,C_{1,H},C_{2,H},C_{G},K_{0})>0
. Combining (
7.6
), (
7.13
), and (
7.14
), we deduce that

\displaystyle U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu))+\delta

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}\overline{L}\left(\overline{\gamma}_{s},\dot{\overline{\gamma}}_{s},\mathcal{L}\left(\overline{\gamma}_{s}\right)\right)\,ds\right]-\frac{2C_{2}(1+C_{0}/C)(T-t)^{2}}{N}-C_{0}N\varepsilon

\displaystyle\geq U_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)-\frac{2C_{2}(1+C_{0}/C)(T-t)^{2}}{N}-C_{0}N\varepsilon.

where
C_{2},C_{0},C
are the constants obtained above. Letting
\delta\to 0
and optimizing with respect to
N
we obtain

U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)-U_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)\geq-C_{1}\left(2(T-t)+\frac{M_{0}}{T-t}\right)\sqrt{\varepsilon},

for some constant
C_{1}>0
depending only on
C_{2},C
considered above and hence on
d,C_{1,H},C_{2,H},C_{G}
and
K_{0}
. With a similar argument, as in the proof of Theorem
1.2
, we establish

U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)-U_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)\leq C_{1}^{\prime}\left(2(T-t)+\frac{M_{0}}{T-t}\right)\sqrt{\varepsilon},

for some constant
C_{1}^{\prime}>0
depending only on
d,C_{1,H},C_{2,H},C_{G}
and
K_{0}
. We omit the details of the last calculation. The first result follows.

Step 3.
(Convergence and rate of convergence under (A1’))

Fix
(t,\mu,\nu)\in[0,T)\times\mathcal{P}_{2}\times\mathcal{P}_{2}
. Combining the equality
U^{\varepsilon}_{1}(t,\mu)=\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}}\left\{U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)+F(\nu)\right\}
with the equality of Lemma
4.5
, we obtain

\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}}\left\{U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)+F(\nu)\right\}=\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}}\left\{\inf_{(X,Y)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\nu}}\mathbb{E}[h^{\varepsilon}(t,T,X,Y)]+F(\nu)\right\},

for any bounded and
{\bf d}_{2}
-Lipschitz terminal cost
F:\mathcal{P}_{2}\to\mathbb{R}
. By the continuity and the quadratic growth estimates proved for
h^{\varepsilon}
in Proposition
2.5
, we derive from
[
V+09
, Theorem 5.20]
that the optimal transport value function
\nu\mapsto\inf_{(X,Y)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\nu}}\mathbb{E}[h^{\varepsilon}(t,T,X,Y)]
is continuous. Thus, as in Step 1 by Lemma
7.2
, we have

U^{\varepsilon}_{ot}(t,\mu,\nu)=U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)=\inf_{(X,Y)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\nu}}\mathbb{E}[h^{\varepsilon}(t,T,X,Y)].

We can similarly show that

U_{ot}(t,\mu,\nu)=U_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)=\inf_{(X,Y)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\nu}}\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}(t,T,X,Y)],

where
\overline{h}
is from Proposition
2.6
. We finish the proof by applying the estimate for the difference of
\overline{h},h^{\varepsilon}
from Proposition
2.6
(3):

\displaystyle\inf_{(X,Y)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\nu}}\mathbb{E}[h^{\varepsilon}(t,T,X,Y)]

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{(X,Y)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\nu}}\left\{\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}(t,T,X,Y)]-\varepsilon C\left(1+\frac{\mathbb{E}[|X-Y|^{2}]}{(T-t)^{2}}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{(X,Y)\in\mathcal{E}_{\mu,\nu}}\left\{\mathbb{E}[\overline{h}(t,T,X,Y)]-\varepsilon C\left(1+\frac{{\bf d}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\nu)}{(T-t)^{2}}\right)\right\},

for some constant
C=C(d,C_{1,H},C_{2,H},K_{0})>0
. Thus,
U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)\geq U_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)-\varepsilon C\left(1+\frac{{\bf d}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\nu)}{(T-t)^{2}}\right)
. We show
U_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)\geq U^{\varepsilon}_{1,ot}(t,\mu,\nu)-\varepsilon C\left(1+\frac{{\bf d}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\nu)}{(T-t)^{2}}\right)
similarly.
∎

Appendix A
Proofs of preliminary results

Proof of Proposition
2.1
.

We only prove (1) and (4). The proof of the remaining statements can be found in
[
Tra21
]
.

(1) Fix
y\in\mathbb{T}^{d},x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
m\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
. By (
2.2
),

0\cdot v-H(y,x,0,m)\geq-K_{0}.

Moreover, again by (
2.2
), for any
p\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\displaystyle|p|\geq\frac{|v|+2K_{0}}{C_{1,H}}+1
,

-p\cdot v-H(y,x,p,m)\leq|p|\,|v|-C_{1,H}|p|^{2}+K_{0}\leq|p|\left(|v|-C_{1,H}|p|\right)+K_{0}\leq-K_{0}.

Therefore,

L(y,x,v,m)=\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\big(-p\cdot v-H(y,x,p,m)\big)=\max_{|p|\leq\frac{|v|+2K_{0}}{C_{1,H}}+1}\big(-p\cdot v-H(y,x,p,m)\big)<+\infty.

(4) Let
y,y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
,
x,x^{\prime},v,v^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. From part (1), there exists
p_{x,y,m}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
|p_{x,y,m}|\leq\frac{|v|+2K_{0}}{C_{1,H}}+1
such that

L(y,x,v,m)=-p_{x,y,m}\cdot v-H(y,x,p_{x,y,m},m).

We estimate:

\displaystyle L(y,x,v,m)-L(y^{\prime},x^{\prime},v^{\prime},m^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq-p_{x,y,m}\cdot v-H(y,x,p_{x,y,m},m)-\big(-p_{x,y,m}\cdot v^{\prime}-H(y^{\prime},x^{\prime},p_{x,y,m},m^{\prime})\big)

\displaystyle=H(y^{\prime},x^{\prime},p_{x,y,m},m^{\prime})-H(y,x,p_{x,y,m},m)+p_{x,y,m}\cdot(v^{\prime}-v)

\displaystyle\leq H(y,x,p_{x,y,m},m^{\prime})-H(y,x,p_{x,y,m},m)+\left(\frac{|v|+2K_{0}}{C_{1,H}}+1\right)|v-v^{\prime}|.

\displaystyle\leq C_{H}\left(1+2\left(\frac{|v|+2K_{0}}{C_{1,H}}+1\right)\right)\left(|x-x^{\prime}|+|y-y^{\prime}|+{\bf d}_{2}(m,m^{\prime})\right)+\left(\frac{|v|+2K_{0}}{C_{1,H}}+1\right)|v-v^{\prime}|

\displaystyle\leq C\left(1+|v|\right)\left(|x-x^{\prime}|+|y-y^{\prime}|+|v-v^{\prime}|+{\bf d}_{2}(m,m^{\prime})\right),

where the second-to-last inequality follows from (
2.3
) and
C=C\left(C_{1,H},C_{H},K_{0}\right)>0
.

By symmetry, exchanging
(x,v)
and
(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})
gives

L(y^{\prime},x^{\prime},v^{\prime},m^{\prime})-L(y,x,v,m)\leq C\left(1+|v^{\prime}|\right)\left(|x-x^{\prime}|+|y-y^{\prime}|+|v-v^{\prime}|+{\bf d}_{2}(m,m^{\prime})\right).

Hence,

\left|L(y,x,v,m)-L(y^{\prime},x^{\prime},v^{\prime},m^{\prime})\right|\leq C_{L}\left(1+|v|+|v^{\prime}|\right)\left(|x-x^{\prime}|+|v-v^{\prime}|\right),

for some constant
C_{L}=C_{L}\left(C_{1,H},C_{H},K_{0}\right)>0
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2.2
.

We only prove (
2.12
) and (
2.13
); the remaining assertions follow from the proof of
[
Tra21
, Theorems 4.3, 4.14]
.

From
[
Tra21
, (4.17)]
, we know that

\min_{y\in\mathbb{T}^{d}}H(y,x,p,m)\leq\overline{H}(x,p,m)\leq\max_{y\in\mathbb{T}^{d}}H(y,x,p,m),\qquad\text{for all }x,p\in\mathbb{R}^{d},m\in\mathcal{P}_{2}.

Together with (
2.2
), this yields

(A.1)

C_{1,H}|p|^{2}-K_{0}\leq\overline{H}(x,p,m)\leq C_{2,H}|p|^{2}+K_{0}.

Let
x^{\prime},p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
m^{\prime}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
, and
u^{\delta}:\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
be the unique solution of

\delta u^{\delta}(y)+H\left(y,x^{\prime},Du^{\delta}(y)+p^{\prime},m^{\prime}\right)=0,\qquad y\in\mathbb{T}^{d}.

As
-\delta u^{\delta}(\cdot)\to\overline{H}(x^{\prime},p^{\prime},m^{\prime})
uniformly on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
, for
\delta>0
small enough, by (
A.1
) and (
2.2
), we have

C_{1,H}\left|Du^{\delta}+p^{\prime}\right|^{2}-K_{0}\leq H(y,x^{\prime},Du^{\delta}+p^{\prime},m^{\prime})=-\delta u^{\delta}(y)\leq\overline{H}(p^{\prime},m^{\prime})+1\leq C_{2,H}|p|^{2}+K_{0}+1,

which implies

\left|Du^{\delta}+p^{\prime}\right|^{2}\leq\frac{C_{2,H}}{C_{1,H}}|p^{\prime}|^{2}+\frac{2K_{0}+1}{C_{1,H}}.

Hence,

(A.2)

\left|Du^{\delta}\right|\leq C\left(1+\left|p^{\prime}\right|\right)

for some constant
C=C\left(C_{1,H},C_{2,H},K_{0}\right)>0
.
We estimate

(A.3)

\displaystyle\left|H\left(y,x^{\prime},Du^{\delta}(y)+p^{\prime},m^{\prime}\right)-H\left(y,x,Du^{\delta}(y)+p,m\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{H}\left(1+2\left|Du^{\delta}\right|+|p|+|p^{\prime}|\right)\left(\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|+\left|p-p^{\prime}\right|+{\bf d}_{2}(m,m^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\overline{H}}\left(1+|p|+|p^{\prime}|\right)\left(\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|+\left|p-p^{\prime}\right|+{\bf d}_{2}(m,m^{\prime})\right),

where
C_{\overline{H}}=C_{\overline{H}}\left(C_{1,H},C_{2,H},C_{H},K_{0}\right)>0
is a constant, and the second inequality follows from (
A.2
). Consider
\displaystyle u^{\delta}+\frac{C_{\overline{H}}\left(1+|p|+|p^{\prime}|\right)\left(\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|+\left|p-p^{\prime}\right|+{\bf d}_{2}(m,m^{\prime})\right)}{\delta}
. This is a supersolution to (
2.10
), since by (
A.3
),

\displaystyle\delta u^{\delta}+C_{\overline{H}}\left(1+|p|+|p^{\prime}|\right)\left(\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|+\left|p-p^{\prime}\right|+{\bf d}_{2}(m,m^{\prime})\right)+H\left(y,x,Du^{\delta}(y)+p,m\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\delta u^{\delta}+H\left(y,x^{\prime},Du^{\delta}(y)+p^{\prime},m^{\prime}\right)=0.

Similarly,
\displaystyle u^{\delta}-\frac{C_{\overline{H}}\left(1+|p|+|p^{\prime}|\right)\left(\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|+\left|p-p^{\prime}\right|+{\bf d}_{2}(m,m^{\prime})\right)}{\delta}
is a subsolution to (
2.10
). By the comparison principle for (
2.10
), we obtain

\displaystyle u^{\delta}-\frac{C_{\overline{H}}\left(1+|p|+|p^{\prime}|\right)\left(\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|+\left|p-p^{\prime}\right|+{\bf d}_{2}(m,m^{\prime})\right)}{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq v^{\delta}\leq u^{\delta}+\frac{C_{\overline{H}}\left(1+|p|+|p^{\prime}|\right)\left(\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|+\left|p-p^{\prime}\right|+{\bf d}_{2}(m,m^{\prime})\right)}{\delta}.

Multiplying by
\delta
and sending
\delta\to 0
, we deduce

\left|\overline{H}(x,p,m)-\overline{H}(x^{\prime},p^{\prime},m^{\prime})\right|\leq C_{\overline{H}}\left(1+|p|+|p^{\prime}|\right)\left(\left|x-x^{\prime}\right|+\left|p-p^{\prime}\right|+{\bf d}_{2}(m,m^{\prime})\right).

∎

Proof of Proposition
2.6
, parts (1) and (3).

We begin by proving part (1). Without loss of generality, we assume

t_{1}=0
,
t_{2}=t
for some
t\in(0,T]
, and
x=0
. The general case
follows by the same argument.

Since, by definition,
h(0,2t,0,2y)\leq h(0,t,0,y)+h(0,t,y,2y)
, it suffices to show

(A.4)

h(0,t,y,2y)\leq h(0,t,0,y)+C\left(1+\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}\right)

for some constant
C=C(d,C_{1,H},C_{2,H},K_{0})>0
.

Consider the straight line
\gamma:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
connecting
y
and
2y
, defined by
\gamma(s)=\frac{s}{t}y+y
. By the definition of
h(0,t,y,2y)
,

(A.5)

h(0,t,y,2y)\leq\int_{0}^{t}L\left(\frac{s}{t}y+y,\frac{y}{t}\right)\,ds\leq\left(\frac{1}{4C_{1,H}}\cdot\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}+K_{0}\right)t,

where the second inequality follows from (
2.8
).

Let
\eta:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a minimizing curve of
h(0,t,0,y)
, that is,
\eta(0)=0,\eta(t)=y
, and

(A.6)

h(0,t,0,y)=\int_{0}^{t}L\left(\eta(s),\dot{\eta}(s)\right)ds\geq-K_{0}t,

where the second inequality follows from (
2.8
). Consider the straight line
\tilde{\gamma}:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
connecting
0
and
y
, defined by
\tilde{\gamma}(s)=\frac{s}{t}y
. Then

(A.7)

h(0,t,0,y)=\int_{0}^{t}L\left(\eta(s),\dot{\eta}(s)\right)ds\leq\int_{0}^{t}L\left(\frac{s}{t}y,\frac{y}{t}\right)\,ds\leq\left(\frac{1}{4C_{1,H}}\cdot\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}+K_{0}\right)t,

where the last inequality follows from (
2.8
).

If
t\leq 4
, then by (
A.5
) and (
A.6
) we have

h(0,t,y,2y)\leq\frac{1}{C_{1,H}}\cdot\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}+4K_{0},\qquad h(0,t,0,y)\geq-4K_{0}.

Consequently,

h(0,t,y,2y)\leq h(0,t,0,y)+\frac{1}{C_{1,H}}\cdot\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}+8K_{0},

and therefore (
A.4
) holds.

Suppose
t>4
. We claim that there exists
d\in\left\{0,1,\cdots,\lfloor t\rfloor-1\right\}
such that

(A.8)

\frac{1}{4C_{2,H}}\int_{d}^{d+1}\left|\dot{\eta}(s)\right|^{2}\,ds\leq\frac{1}{C_{1,H}}\cdot\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}+4K_{0}.

Indeed, if no such
d
existed, then

\frac{1}{4C_{2,H}}\int_{0}^{\lfloor t\rfloor}\left|\dot{\eta}(s)\right|^{2}\,ds\geq\lfloor t\rfloor\left(\frac{1}{C_{1,H}}\cdot\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}+4K_{0}\right).

Combining this with (
A.7
), we obtain

(A.9)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{4C_{1,H}}\cdot\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}+K_{0}\right)t

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{\lfloor t\rfloor}L\left(\eta(s),\dot{\eta}(s)\right)ds+\int_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{t}L\left(\eta(s),\dot{\eta}(s)\right)ds

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4C_{2,H}}\int_{0}^{\lfloor t\rfloor}\left|\dot{\eta}(s)\right|^{2}\,ds-K_{0}t

\displaystyle\geq\lfloor t\rfloor\left(\frac{1}{C_{1,H}}\cdot\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}+4K_{0}\right)-K_{0}t,

where the second inequality follows from (
2.8
). Since
t>4
and
\lfloor t\rfloor-t>-1
, we have
4\lfloor t\rfloor-2t\geq 2\lfloor t\rfloor-2>0
, and hence

\displaystyle\lfloor t\rfloor\left(\frac{1}{C_{1,H}}\cdot\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}+4K_{0}\right)-K_{0}t

\displaystyle=\lfloor t\rfloor\left(\frac{1}{C_{1,H}}\cdot\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}\right)+(4\lfloor t\rfloor-t)K_{0}

\displaystyle>\frac{t}{4}\left(\frac{1}{C_{1,H}}\cdot\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}\right)+tK_{0},

which contradicts (
A.9
). Hence, (
A.8
) holds.

Let
w\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
such that
y-w\in[0,1]^{d}
. Define a path
\tilde{\eta}:[0,t]\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
by

\tilde{\eta}(s):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &w+4\left(\frac{1}{4}-s\right)(y-w),\qquad\qquad\quad\,\,\,\text{if}\,\,\,0\leq s\leq\frac{1}{4},\\
&\eta\left(s-\frac{1}{4}\right)+w,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\text{if}\,\,\,\frac{1}{4}\leq s\leq d+\frac{1}{4},\\
&\eta\left(2\left(s-d-\frac{1}{4}\right)+d\right)+w,\qquad\quad\,\,\text{if}\,\,\,d+\frac{1}{4}\leq s\leq d+\frac{3}{4}\\
&\eta\left(s+\frac{1}{4}\right)+w,\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\text{if}\,\,\,d+\frac{3}{4}\leq s\leq t-\frac{1}{4},\\
&y+w+4\left(s-t+\frac{1}{4}\right)\left(y-w\right),\qquad\text{if}\,\,\,t-\frac{1}{4}\leq s\leq t.\\
\end{aligned}\right.

Since
\tilde{\eta}(0)=y
and
\tilde{\eta}(t)=2y
,
\tilde{\eta}
is an admissible path for
h(0,t,y,2y)
. Thus,

(A.10)

h(0,t,y,2y)\leq\int_{0}^{t}L\left(\tilde{\eta}(s),\dot{\tilde{\eta}}(s)\right)\,ds.

We decompose the integral as

(A.11)

\int_{0}^{t}L\left(\tilde{\eta}(s),\dot{\tilde{\eta}}(s)\right)\,ds=\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}+\mathrm{III},

where

\mathrm{I}:=\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}L\left(\tilde{\eta}(s),\dot{\tilde{\eta}}(s)\right)\,ds+\int_{t-\frac{1}{4}}^{t}L\left(\tilde{\eta}(s),\dot{\tilde{\eta}}(s)\right)\,ds,

\mathrm{II}:=\int_{\frac{1}{4}}^{d+\frac{1}{4}}L\left(\tilde{\eta}(s),\dot{\tilde{\eta}}(s)\right)\,ds+\int_{d+\frac{3}{4}}^{t-\frac{1}{4}}L\left(\tilde{\eta}(s),\dot{\tilde{\eta}}(s)\right)\,ds,\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{III}:=\int_{d+\frac{1}{4}}^{d+\frac{3}{4}}L\left(\tilde{\eta}(s),\dot{\tilde{\eta}}(s)\right)\,ds.

We first estimate
\mathrm{I}
. By the definition of
\tilde{\eta}
,

(A.12)

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{4}}L\left(w+4\left(\frac{1}{4}-s\right)(y-w),-4(y-w)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle+\int_{t-\frac{1}{4}}^{t}L\left(y+w+4\left(s-t+\frac{1}{4}\right)\left(y-w\right),4(y-w)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left(\frac{1}{4C_{1,H}}\left(4\sqrt{d}\right)^{2}+K_{0}\right)\cdot 2,

where the inequality follows from (
2.8
) and the fact that
|y-w|\leq\sqrt{d}
since
y-w\in[0,1]^{d}
.
Next, we estimate
\mathrm{II}
:

(A.13)

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}=

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{1}{4}}^{d+\frac{1}{4}}L\left(\eta\left(s-\frac{1}{4}\right)+w,\dot{\eta}\left(s-\frac{1}{4}\right)\right)\,ds+\int_{d+\frac{3}{4}}^{t-\frac{1}{4}}L\left(\eta\left(s+\frac{1}{4}\right)+w,\dot{\eta}\left(s+\frac{1}{4}\right)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{d}L\left(\eta(s),\dot{\eta}(s)\right)\,ds+\int_{d+1}^{t}L\left(\eta\left(s\right),\dot{\eta}\left(s\right)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}L\left(\eta(s),\dot{\eta}(s)\right)\,ds-\int_{d}^{d+1}L\left(\eta\left(s\right),\dot{\eta}\left(s\right)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle h(0,t,0,y)+K_{0},

where the second line follows from the fact that
x\mapsto L(x,v)
is periodic for every
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and the last inequality follows from (
2.8
). Finally, we estimate
\mathrm{III}
:

(A.14)

\displaystyle\mathrm{III}=

\displaystyle\int_{d+\frac{1}{4}}^{d+\frac{3}{4}}L\left(\eta\left(2\left(s-d-\frac{1}{4}\right)+d\right)+w,2\dot{\eta}\left(2\left(s-d-\frac{1}{4}\right)+d\right)\right)\,ds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{d}^{d+1}L\left(\eta\left(s\right),2\dot{\eta}\left(s\right)\right)\,ds\leq\frac{1}{2C_{1,H}}\int_{d}^{d+1}\left|\dot{\eta}(s)\right|^{2}\,ds+\frac{K_{0}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{2,H}}{\left(C_{1,H}\right)^{2}}\cdot\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}+\left(\frac{8C_{2,H}}{C_{1,H}}+\frac{1}{2}\right)K_{0},

where the last inequality follows from (
A.8
). Now combining (
A.10
), (
A.11
), (
A.12
), (
A.13
), and (
A.14
), we have

h(0,t,y,2y)\leq h(0,t,0,y)+\frac{2C_{2,H}}{(C_{1,H})^{2}}\cdot\frac{|y|^{2}}{t^{2}}+\left(\frac{8C_{2,H}}{C_{1,H}}+2\right)K_{0}+\frac{2d}{C_{1,H}}.

This completes the proof of part (1).

Now we prove part (3). By part (1) and Fekete’s lemma,
\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0^{+}}\varepsilon m\left(\frac{t_{1}}{\varepsilon},\frac{t_{2}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon},\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)
exists. For simplicity, set
C^{\prime}:=C\left(1+\frac{|y-x|^{2}}{(t_{2}-t_{1})^{2}}\right)
. By subadditivity, we have

h(2t_{1},2t_{2},2x,2y)+C^{\prime} \leq 2(h(t_{1},t_{2},x,y)+C^{\prime}).

Iterating this inequality
k
times yields

h\left(2^{k}t_{1},2^{k}t_{2},2^{k}x,2^{k}y\right)+C^{\prime}\leq 2^{k}\left(h(t_{1},t_{2},x,y)+C^{\prime}\right).

Dividing both sides by
2^{k}
and letting
k\to\infty
, we obtain

\overline{h}(t_{1},t_{2},x,y)\leq h(t_{1},t_{2},x,y)+C^{\prime}.

Replacing
(t_{1},t_{2},x,y)
by
\left(\frac{t_{1}}{\varepsilon},\frac{t_{2}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon},\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)
and multiplying by
\varepsilon
gives

\varepsilon\overline{h}\left(\frac{t_{1}}{\varepsilon},\frac{t_{2}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon},\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\leq\varepsilon h\left(\frac{t_{1}}{\varepsilon},\frac{t_{2}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon},\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)+\varepsilon C^{\prime}.

Note that the left-hand side equals
\overline{h}(t_{1},t_{2},x,y)
and hence,

\overline{h}(t_{1},t_{2},x,y)-\varepsilon h\left(\frac{t_{1}}{\varepsilon},\frac{t_{2}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon},\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\leq C\left(1+\frac{|y-x|^{2}}{(t_{2}-t_{1})^{2}}\right)\varepsilon.

The lower bound

\overline{h}(t_{1},t_{2},x,y)-\varepsilon h\left(\frac{t_{1}}{\varepsilon},\frac{t_{2}}{\varepsilon},\frac{x}{\varepsilon},\frac{y}{\varepsilon}\right)\geq-C\left(1+\frac{|y-x|^{2}}{(t_{2}-t_{1})^{2}}\right)\varepsilon

follows analogously from superadditivity.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of Corollary
1.5

Proof of Corollary
1.5
.

We first prove (1). We observe that for any
\alpha\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
and any
s\in[t,T]
,
X_{s}=X_{t}+\int_{t}^{s}\alpha_{r}dr
is
\sigma(X_{t})
-measurable, hence
\mathcal{L}(X_{T})
is some pushforward if
m=\mathcal{L}(X_{t})
. The techniques of Proposition
4.4
yield

\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(t,m)=\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}}\left\{\inf_{f:f_{\#}m=\nu}\left\{\int h^{\varepsilon}(t,T,x,f(x))m(dx)\right\}+G(\nu)\right\},

which is associated with the Monge formulation of the optimal transport problem with cost function
h^{\varepsilon}(t,T,\cdot,\cdot)\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
. Recall
U_{1}^{\varepsilon}
introduced at the beginning of section
4.1
. By Lemma
4.5
, we have

\displaystyle U_{1}^{\varepsilon}(t,m)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}}\left\{\inf_{(X,Y)\in\mathcal{E}_{m,\nu}}\mathbb{E}[h^{\varepsilon}(t,T,X,Y)]+G(\nu)\right\}

\displaystyle=\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}}\left\{\inf_{\pi\in\Pi(m,\nu)}\iint h^{\varepsilon}(t,T,x,y)\pi(dx,dy)+G(\nu)\right\},

which is associated with the Kantorovich formulation of the optimal transport problem. Since, by Proposition
4.7
,
U^{\varepsilon}_{1}=U^{\varepsilon}\leq\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}
, we derive

(B.1)

\begin{split}&\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}}\left\{\inf_{\pi\in\Pi(m,\nu)}\iint h^{\varepsilon}(t,T,x,y)\pi(dx,dy)+G(\nu)\right\}\leq U^{\varepsilon}(t,m)\\
&\hskip 142.26378pt\leq\inf_{\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}}\left\{\inf_{f:f_{\#}m=\nu}\left\{\int h^{\varepsilon}(t,T,x,f(x))m(dx)\right\}+G(\nu)\right\}.\end{split}

Since
h^{\varepsilon}(t,T,x,y)
is continuous, lower bounded and has quadratic growth with respect to
x,y
(see Propositions
2.5
and
2.6
), we can apply the result of Lacker-Beiglbock
[
BL18
, Proposition 2.11]
or Prateli
[
Pra07
, Theorem B]
to derive that the Monge-Kantorovich optimal transport problems are equal and hence (
B.1
) holds as chains of equalities if
m
is non-atomic.

Now we first prove (2) using proof by contradiction. Assume
U^{\varepsilon}(t,\delta_{x_{0}})<\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(t,\delta_{x_{0}})
for some
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Recall the definiton of
U^{\varepsilon}_{2}
in section
4.1
. By Proposition
4.7
, we have
U^{\varepsilon}_{2}(t,\delta_{x_{0}})=U^{\varepsilon}(t,\delta_{x_{0}})<\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(t,\delta_{x_{0}})
. According to the definition of
\mathcal{A}_{2}
, there exists
\alpha\in\mathcal{A}_{2}
such that

(B.2)

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}}{\varepsilon},\alpha\left(s,\xi(\omega)\right)\right)ds+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g(x)\mathrm{d}m_{T}(\mathrm{d}x)\right]<\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(t,\delta_{x_{0}}),

We define
X_{s}(\omega)
as the solution to the ODE

dX_{s}(\omega)=\alpha\left(s,\xi(\omega)\right)ds,\quad s\in[t,T],\quad X_{t}(\omega)=x_{0}.

(
B.2
) implies that

\mathbb{E}_{\omega}\left[\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}(\omega)}{\varepsilon},\alpha\left(s,\xi(\omega)\right)\right)ds+g(X_{T}(\omega))\right]<\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(t,\delta_{x_{0}})

Thus, there exists
\omega_{0}\in\Omega
such that

X_{t}(\omega_{0})=x_{0},\quad\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}(\omega_{0})}{\varepsilon},\alpha\left(s,\xi(\omega_{0})\right)\right)ds+g(X_{T}(\omega_{0}))<\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(t,\delta_{x_{0}}).

Now, we define
\tilde{\alpha}(s):=\alpha\left(s,\xi(\omega_{0})\right)
for all
s\in[t,T]
. We observe that
\tilde{\alpha}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
. Let
\tilde{X}_{s}
be the solution to the ODE with control
\tilde{\alpha}
, that is,
d\tilde{X}_{s}=\tilde{\alpha}(s)ds
for
s\in[t,T]
and
\tilde{X}_{t}=x_{0}
. Then,

\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{X_{s}(\omega_{0})}{\varepsilon},\alpha\left(s,\xi(\omega_{0})\right)\right)ds+g(X_{T}(\omega_{0}))=\int_{t}^{T}L\left(\frac{\tilde{X}_{s}}{\varepsilon},\tilde{\alpha}(s)\right)ds+g(X_{T})<\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(t,\delta_{x_{0}})

This is a contradiction to the definition of
\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(t,\delta_{x_{0}})
. Hence,
U^{\varepsilon}(t,\delta_{x_{0}})=\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(t,\delta_{x_{0}})
. We conclude the proof.
∎

The equality
U^{\varepsilon}(t,m)=\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(t,m)
does not necessarily hold if
m
has atoms (see
[
CDJM25
, Corollary 6.10]
). This hints at the fact that
\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(t,\cdot)
might be discontinuous at some non-atomic measures. We now present such an example.

Example of discontinuous
\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}
.
Consider
d=1
,
t=0,\;T=1
,
G(\nu)={\bf d}_{1}(\nu,\text{Leb}_{|[0,1]})
,
L(x,v)=|v|^{2}/2
and
m=\delta_{0}
.

Since the dynamics from
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
are deterministic given
X_{0}
, we have that
(X_{s})_{s\in[0,1]}
is a deterministic curve in
\mathbb{R}
starting from
0
and we compute

\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(0,\delta_{0})=\inf_{y\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\frac{|y|^{2}}{2}+\int_{0}^{1}|y-x|dx\right\}=\frac{1}{3}.

We consider the sequence of measures

m_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\delta_{k/n^{2}}\xrightarrow{{\bf d_{2}}}\delta_{0}

and the control
\alpha^{n}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
with
\alpha^{n}(s,\frac{k}{n^{2}})=\frac{k}{n}-\frac{k}{n^{2}}
. We observe that
X_{1}=\frac{k}{n}
with probability
\frac{1}{n}
, so
\mathcal{L}(X_{1}^{\alpha^{n}})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\delta_{k/n}
. We have

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(0,m_{n})\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{1}\frac{|\alpha^{n}_{s}|^{2}}{2}ds+G(\mathcal{L}(X_{1}^{\alpha^{n}}))\right]=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\frac{k}{n}-\frac{k}{n^{2}}\right)^{2}+{\bf d}_{1}(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\delta_{k/n},\text{Leb}_{|[0,1]}).

The last expression converges as
n\to+\infty
to
\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}x^{2}dx=\frac{1}{6}<\frac{1}{3}
. Hence,
\limsup_{n}\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(0,m_{n})<\widetilde{U}^{\varepsilon}(0,\delta_{0})
.

Appendix C
Technical proofs

Proof of Proposition
3.1
.

The second estimate (
3.2
) follows as in
[
GMS21
, Theorem 3.3]
(note that we may assume that
\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|_{2-r}/N^{\frac{1}{2-r}}=(\sum_{i=1}^{N}(x_{i}-y_{i})^{2-r})^{\frac{1}{2-r}}/N^{\frac{1}{2-r}}={\bf d}_{2-r}(m^{N}_{\bm{x}},m^{N}_{\bm{y}})
because
V^{N}_{\varepsilon}(t,\bm{x})
is symmetric in
x_{i},x_{j}
since
G(m^{N}_{\bm{x}})
is symmetric). To prove (
3.1
) we argue as follows.

Since
G
is
{\bf d}_{2}
-Lipschitz, by
[
CM23
, Theorem 4.2]
, we may find a sequence of functions
G_{k}\colon\mathcal{P}_{2}\to\mathbb{R}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, which are smooth and
{\bf d}_{2}
-Lispchitz continuous with the same Lipschitz constant as
G
such that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\sup_{m\in B}|G_{k}(m)-G(m)|=0,

for every compact set
B\subset\mathcal{P}_{2}
. We will show the desired estimate when the terminal condition in (

HJB
N

) is
V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(T,\bm{x})=G_{k}(m^{N}_{\bm{x}})
for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
and then the result will follow by sending
k\to\infty
due to the stability of viscosity solutions.
Let
m\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
,
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
measurable with at most linear growth and set
m_{t}:=(\text{Id}+t\phi)_{\#}m
, for
t>0
. We have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{G_{k}(m_{t})-G_{k}(m)}{t}\right|\leq\frac{C_{G}}{t}{\bf d}_{2}(m_{t},m)\leq\frac{C_{G}}{t}\|t\phi\|_{L^{2}_{m}}=C_{G}\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{m}}.

Sending
t\to 0^{+}
yields

\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}D_{m}G_{k}(m,x)\cdot\phi(x)m(dx)\right|\leq C_{G}\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{m}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi\text{ arbitrary}}}{{\implies}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|D_{m}G_{k}(m,x)|^{2}m(dx)\leq C_{G}.

Therefore, if
m=m^{N}_{\bm{x}}
for some
\bm{x}\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{N}
, we derive

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|D_{m}G(m^{N}_{\bm{x}},x^{i})|^{2}\leq C_{G}.

For convenience, we reverse the time and we show the result for the unique solution
V_{\varepsilon,k}^{N}\colon[0,+\infty)\times(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{N}\to\mathbb{R}
of the PDE

(C.1)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}H(\frac{x^{i}}{\varepsilon},x^{i},ND_{x^{i}}V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k},m_{\bm{x}}^{N})=0,&(t,\bm{x})\in(0,+\infty)\times(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{N},\\
V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(0,\bm{x})=G_{k}(m_{\bm{x}}^{N}),&\bm{x}\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{N}.\end{cases}

Note that, in view of (
2.6
), for all
\varepsilon>0
and
\bm{x}\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{N}
we have

(C.2)

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}H\left(\frac{x^{i}}{\varepsilon},D_{m}G(m^{N}_{\bm{x}},x^{i}),m_{\bm{x}}^{N}\right)\right|\leq C_{H}\frac{N+\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|D_{m}G_{k}(m^{N}_{\bm{x}},x^{i})\right|^{2}}{N}\leq 1+C_{G}.

This implies that
V^{N,+}_{\varepsilon,k}(t,\bm{x})=Ct+G_{k}(m^{N}_{\bm{x}})
and
V^{N,-}_{\varepsilon,k}(t,\bm{x})=-Ct+G_{k}(m^{N}_{\bm{x}})
are a classical supersolution and a classical subsolution of (
C.1
), for some constant
C>0
large enough and independent of
N,\varepsilon,k
, respectively. By comparison principle, we get

-Ct+G_{k}(m^{N}_{\bm{x}})\leq V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(t,\bm{x})\leq Ct+G_{k}(m^{N}_{\bm{x}}),\;\text{for all }(t,\bm{x})\in(0,+\infty)\times(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{N},

which gives the Lipschitz regularity in time at
t=0
. To get the Lipschitz regularity in time for any
s>0
, we notice that
(t,\bm{x})\mapsto V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(s+t,\bm{x})
is also a viscosity solution of (
C.1
) with initial condition
V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(s,\bm{x})
. Then, in light of the inequality

V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(s,\bm{x})-\|G_{k}(m^{N}_{\cdot})-V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(s,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\leq G_{k}(m^{N}_{\bm{x}})\leq V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(s,\bm{x})+\|G_{k}(m^{N}_{\cdot})-V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(s,\cdot)\|_{\infty}

and the comparison principle, we get

V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(t+s,\bm{x})-\|G_{k}(m^{N}_{\cdot})-V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(s,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\leq V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(t,\bm{x})\leq V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(t+s,\bm{x})+\|G_{k}(m^{N}_{\cdot})-V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(s,\cdot)\|_{\infty}

or

\left|V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(t+s,\bm{x})-V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(t,\bm{x})\right|\leq\|G_{k}(m^{N}_{\cdot})-V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(s,\cdot)\|_{\infty}\leq Cs,

for any
(t,\bm{x})\in[0,+\infty)\times(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{N}
.

We further know that
V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}
is Lipschitz continuous in both variables (see
[
Tra21
, Theorem 1.34]
), therefore
V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}
satisfies (
C.1
) a.e. Thus, we have from the Lipschitz continuity with respect to
t

\displaystyle C\geq|\partial_{t}V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(t,\bm{x})|\geq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}H\left(\frac{x^{i}}{\varepsilon},x^{i},ND_{x^{i}}V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k},m_{\bm{x}}^{N}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{H}}{N}\left(-N+\sum_{i=1}^{N}|ND_{x^{i}}V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(t,\bm{x})|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq-c_{H}+c_{H}N\sum_{i=1}^{N}|D_{x^{i}}V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(t,\bm{x})|^{2},\;\;\text{ a.e.}

This gives a bound of the form
C_{0}\geq N\sum_{i=1}^{N}|D_{x^{i}}V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}|^{2}
almost everywhere. Now let
\bm{x},\bm{y}\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{N}
and
t\geq 0
. Since
V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}
is symmetrical in the
\bm{x}
variables, we may assume that
{\bf d}_{2}^{2}(m^{N}_{\bm{x}},m^{N}_{\bm{y}})=N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}|x^{i}-y^{i}|^{2}=N^{-1}\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|_{2}^{2}
. We have

\displaystyle|V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(t,\bm{x})-V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(t,\bm{y})|=\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\text{d}}{\text{d}s}V^{N}_{\varepsilon,k}(t,s\bm{x}+(1-s)

\displaystyle\bm{y})ds\leq\esssup_{\bm{z}\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{N}}\;\sum_{i=1}^{N}|D_{x^{i}}V^{N}(t,\bm{z})|\;|x^{i}-y^{i}|

\displaystyle\leq\esssup_{\bm{z}\in(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{N}}\;\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}|D_{x^{i}}V^{N}(t,\bm{z})|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\|\bm{x}-\bm{y}\|_{2}=C_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bf d}_{2}(m^{N}_{\bm{x}},m^{N}_{\bm{y}}).

The proof is complete.
∎

Proof of Corollary
3.2
.

The uniform convergence and the viscosity property were explained in the discussion preceding the statement of this Corollary. We now prove (
3.5
).

Fix
t,s\in[0,T]
and
m_{1},m_{2}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
. Let
(\widetilde{\Omega},\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}})
be a probability space supporting two sequence of independent and identically distributed random variables
(X_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\in L^{2}
and
(Y_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\in L^{2}
such that
\mathcal{L}(X_{k})=m_{1}
and
\mathcal{L}(Y_{k})=m_{2}
, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. We consider the empirical random measures

m^{N}_{1}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\delta_{X_{k}}\quad\text{and}\quad m^{N}_{2}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\delta_{Y_{k}}.

By virtue of the Glivenko-Cantelli law of large numbers (see the footnote below for a proof
8
8

8

The proof follows that of
[
ACD+19
, Theorem 1.1]
, which shows the convergence
{\bf d}_{1}(m_{1}^{N},m_{1})\to 0
assuming that
X_{k}\in L^{1}
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. By
[
AGS05
, Remark 7.1.11]
, it suffices to show
\int\varphi(x)m_{1}^{N}(dx)\xrightarrow{N\to+\infty}\int\varphi(x)m_{1}(dx)
for all bounded and continuous
\varphi\colon\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
and
\int|x|^{2}m_{1}^{N}(dx)\xrightarrow{N\to+\infty}\int|x|^{2}m_{1}(dx)
, almost surely. The first limit holds almost surely for each
\varphi
by the law of large numbers. By the discussion in the beginning of
[
AGS05
, Section 5.1]
and a separability argument, we may select the set of measure zero can be chosen independently of
\varphi
. The second limit holds almost surely by the law of large numbers since
X_{k}\in L^{2}
, hence by throwing away a set of measure zero, both limits hold almost surely.
), we know that

(C.3)

{\bf d}_{2}(m_{1}^{N},m_{1})+{\bf d}_{2}(m_{2}^{N},m_{2})\xrightarrow{N\to+\infty}0,\;\;\text{almost surely}

in the probability space
(\widetilde{\Omega},\widetilde{\mathcal{F}},\widetilde{\mathbb{P}})
. We write, due to the triangle inequality,

(C.4)

\begin{split}\left|U^{\varepsilon}(t,m_{1})-U^{\varepsilon}(s,m_{2})\right|\leq&\left|U^{\varepsilon}(t,m_{1})-U_{\varepsilon}^{N}(t,m_{1}^{N})\right|\\
&\qquad+\left|U_{\varepsilon}^{N}(t,m_{1}^{N})-U^{N}_{\varepsilon}(s,m_{2}^{N})\right|+\left|U^{N}_{\varepsilon}(s,m_{2}^{N})-U^{\varepsilon}(s,m_{2})\right|.\end{split}

We observe by the
{\bf d}_{2-r}
-Lipschitz continuity of
U^{N}_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\left|U^{\varepsilon}(t,m_{1})-U_{\varepsilon}^{N}(t,m_{1}^{N})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|U^{\varepsilon}(t,m_{1})-U^{N}_{\varepsilon}(t,m_{1})\right|+\left|U_{\varepsilon}^{N}(t,m_{1})-U^{N}_{\varepsilon}(t,m_{1}^{N})\right|

(C.5)

\displaystyle\leq\left|U^{\varepsilon}(t,m_{1})-U^{N}_{\varepsilon}(t,m_{1})\right|+C{\bf d}_{2-r}(m_{1},m_{1}^{N}).

Similarly,

(C.6)

\displaystyle\left|U^{\varepsilon}(s,m_{2}^{N})-U_{\varepsilon}^{N}(s,m_{2}^{N})\right|\leq\left|U^{\varepsilon}(s,m_{2})-U^{N}_{\varepsilon}(s,m_{2})\right|+C{\bf d}_{2-r}(m_{2},m_{2}^{N}).

By the pointwise convergence
U^{N}_{\varepsilon}\xrightarrow{N\to+\infty}U^{\varepsilon}
, the bounded convergence theorem and (
C.3
), inequalities (
C
) and (
C.6
) imply

(C.7)

\left|U^{\varepsilon}(t,m_{1})-U_{\varepsilon}^{N}(t,m_{1}^{N})\right|+\left|U^{\varepsilon}(s,m_{2}^{N})-U_{\varepsilon}^{N}(s,m_{2}^{N})\right|\xrightarrow{N\to+\infty}0,

since convergence in
{\bf d}_{2}
gives convergence in
{\bf d}_{2-r}
. Finally, by the definition of
U^{N}_{\varepsilon}
in (
3.3
) and (
3.1
) we have

\displaystyle\left|U_{\varepsilon}^{N}(t,m_{1}^{N})-U^{N}_{\varepsilon}(s,m_{2}^{N})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|V_{\varepsilon}^{N}(t,X_{1},\ldots,X_{N})-V^{N}_{\varepsilon}(s,Y_{1},\ldots,Y_{N})\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\left(|t-s|+{\bf d}_{2}(m_{1}^{N},m_{2}^{N})\right)

(C.8)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\xrightarrow{N\to\infty}C_{0}\left(|t-s|+{\bf d}_{2}(m_{1},m_{2})\right),

where the convergence is almost sure. Sending
N\to+\infty
in (
C.4
) , the desired (
3.5
) follows from (
C.7
) and (
C
).
∎

Appendix D
Measurable selection

Lemma D.1
.

Assume (A1) and (A2). Suppose that
0\leq t_{1}<t_{2}\leq T
and fix
\varepsilon>0
. For any
w,y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}
, and sufficiently large
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a measurable path
\gamma_{n}:[t_{1},t_{2}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

\forall\,w,y,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\quad\gamma(\cdot,w,y,z)\in\mathrm{AC}([t_{1},t_{2}];\mathbb{R}^{d}),\quad\gamma_{n}(t_{1},w,y,z)=y,\quad\gamma_{n}(t_{2},w,y,z)=z,

and

\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}L\left(\frac{\gamma_{n}(t,w,y,z)}{\varepsilon},w,\dot{\gamma}_{n}(t,w,y,z),\mu\right)\,dt<h^{\varepsilon}\left(w,\mu;t_{1},t_{2},y,z\right)+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left(1+|y-z|^{2}\right),

where
C=C(\varepsilon,C_{1,H},C_{2,H},C_{H},K_{0},t_{2}-t_{1})>0
.

Proof.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
and consider the lattice
\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. For each
i,j,k\in\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{Z}^{d}
, let
\gamma_{\left(i,j,k\right)}\in{\rm AC}\left([t_{1},t_{2}];\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)
satisfy

\gamma_{\left(i,j,k\right)}\in\operatorname*{argmin}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\text{AC}\left([t_{1},t_{2}];\mathbb{R}^{d}\right)\\
\gamma(t_{1})=j,\gamma(t_{2})=k\end{subarray}}\int^{t_{2}}_{t_{1}}L\left(\frac{\gamma(t)}{\varepsilon},i,\dot{\gamma}(t),\mu\right)dt\,,

that is,

h^{\varepsilon}\left(i,\mu;t_{1},t_{2},j,k\right)=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}L\left(\frac{\gamma_{\left(i,j,k\right)}(t)}{\varepsilon},i,\dot{\gamma}_{\left(i,j,k\right)}(t),\mu\right)\,dt,\quad\ \gamma_{\left(i,j,k\right)}(t_{1})=j,\quad\gamma_{\left(i,j,k\right)}(t_{2})=k.

Define
\phi_{n}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
by

\phi_{n}(x):=\frac{1}{n}\left(\lfloor nx_{1}\rfloor,\lfloor nx_{2}\rfloor,\cdots,\lfloor nx_{d}\rfloor\right).

Then,
\left|\phi_{n}(y)-y\right|\leq\frac{1}{n}
and

(D.1)

\left|\phi_{n}(y)-\phi_{n}\left(z\right)\right|^{2}\leq\left(\left|\phi_{n}(y)-y\right|+\left|y-z\right|+\left|z-\phi_{n}\left(z\right)\right|\right)^{2}\leq\frac{8}{n^{2}}+2\left|y-z\right|^{2}.

Define
\tilde{\gamma}:[t_{1},t_{2}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
by

\tilde{\gamma}(t,w,y,z):=\gamma_{\left(\phi_{n}(w),\phi_{n}(y),\phi_{n}(z)\right)}(t).

For each
(w,y,z)
, the map
t\mapsto\tilde{\gamma}(t,w,y,z)
is absolutely continuous. For each
t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
,
(w,y,z)\mapsto\tilde{\gamma}(t,w,y,z)
is measurable since it is piecewise continuous. Hence
\tilde{\gamma}
is a Carathéodory function, thus jointly measurable in
(t,w,y,z)
by
[
AB06
, Lemma 4.51]
. Since the straight line connecting
\phi_{n}(y)
and
\phi_{n}(z)

\eta(t):=\phi_{n}(y)+\frac{t-t_{1}}{t_{2}-t_{1}}\left(\phi_{n}(z)-\phi_{n}(y)\right)

is an admissible path for
m^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{n}(w),\mu;t_{1},t_{2},\phi_{n}(y),\phi_{n}(z))
. By (
2.8
),

\displaystyle-K_{0}(t_{2}-t_{1})+\frac{1}{C_{2,H}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\left|\dot{\tilde{\gamma}}(t,w,y,z)\right|^{2}\,dt

\displaystyle\leq h^{\varepsilon}(\phi_{n}(w),\mu;t_{1},t_{2},\phi_{n}(y),\phi_{n}(z))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{1,H}}\cdot\frac{\left|\phi_{n}(y)-\phi_{n}(z)\right|^{2}}{(t_{2}-t_{1})}+K_{0}(t_{2}-t_{1})

Hence,

(D.2)

\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\left|\dot{\tilde{\gamma}}(t,w,y,z)\right|^{2}\,dt\leq C(1+|y-z|^{2}),

for some constant
C:=C(C_{1,H},C_{2,H},K_{0},t_{2}-t_{1})>0
. We claim that for
n
large enough, there exists

d\in\left\{t_{1},t_{1}+\frac{1}{n},t_{1}+\frac{2}{n},\cdots,t_{1}+\frac{1}{n}\left(\left\lfloor n\left(t_{2}-t_{1}\right)\right\rfloor-1\right)\right\}

such that

(D.3)

\int_{d}^{d+\frac{1}{n}}\left|\dot{\tilde{\gamma}}(t,w,y,z)\right|^{2}\,dt\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left(1+|y-z|^{2}\right).

If not,

\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+\frac{1}{n}\left\lfloor n\left(t_{2}-t_{1}\right)\right\rfloor}\left|\dot{\tilde{\gamma}}(t,w,y,z)\right|^{2}\,dt\geq\frac{\left\lfloor n\left(t_{2}-t_{1}\right)\right\rfloor C}{\sqrt{n}}\left(1+|y-z|^{2}\right)\geq 4C\left(1+|y-z|^{2}\right),

for
n
large enough, which contradicts (
D.2
).

Define
\gamma_{n}:[t_{1},t_{2}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
by

\gamma_{n}(t,w,y,z)=\left\{\begin{aligned} &y+4n(t-t_{1})(\phi_{n}(y)-y),\quad\text{ if }t\in\left[t_{1},t_{1}+\frac{1}{4n}\right],\\
&\tilde{\gamma}\left(t-\frac{1}{4n},w,y,z\right),\qquad\text{ if }t\in\left[t_{1}+\frac{1}{4n},d+\frac{1}{4n}\right],\\
&\tilde{\gamma}\left(2\left(t-d-\frac{1}{4n}\right)+d,w,y,z\right),\qquad\text{ if }t\in\left[d+\frac{1}{4n},d+\frac{3}{4n}\right],\\
&\tilde{\gamma}\left(t+\frac{1}{4n},w,y,z\right),\qquad\text{ if }t\in\left[d+\frac{3}{4n},t_{2}-\frac{1}{4n}\right],\\
&\phi_{n}(z)+4n\left(t-t_{2}+\frac{1}{4n}\right)(z-\phi_{n}(z)),\quad\text{ if }t\in\left[t_{2}-\frac{1}{4n},t_{2}\right].\end{aligned}\right.

Here, we accelerate the trajectory
\tilde{\gamma}
over the time period
\left[d,d+\frac{1}{n}\right]
to save a total time of
\frac{1}{2n}
, and we use a time interval of length
\frac{1}{4n}
to connect
y
and
\phi_{n}(y)
, and similarly to connect
z
and
\phi_{n}(z)
. Note that
\gamma_{n}(t_{1},w,y,z)=y,\gamma_{n}(t_{2},w,y,z)=z
. Moreover, for each
t
,
(w,y,z)\mapsto\gamma_{n}(t,w,y,z)
is measurable. For each
(w,y,z)
,
t\mapsto\gamma_{n}(t,w,y,z)
is absolutely continuous. Hence,
\gamma_{n}
is a Carathéodory function and is measuarable. Then,

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}L\left(\frac{\gamma_{n}(t,w,y,z)}{\varepsilon},w,\dot{\gamma}_{n}(t,w,y,z),\mu\right)\,dt=\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}+\mathrm{III},

where

\mathrm{I}:=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+\frac{1}{4n}}L\left(\frac{\gamma_{n}}{\varepsilon},w,4n(\phi_{n}(y)-y),\mu\right)\,dt+\int_{t_{2}-\frac{1}{4n}}^{t_{2}}L\left(\frac{\gamma_{n}}{\varepsilon},w,4n(z-\phi_{n}(z)),\mu\right)\,dt,

\mathrm{II}:=\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\setminus[d,d+\frac{1}{n}]}L\left(\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{\varepsilon},w,\dot{\tilde{\gamma}},\mu\right)\,dt,\qquad\mathrm{III}:=\frac{1}{2}\int_{d}^{d+\frac{1}{n}}L\left(\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{\varepsilon},w,2\dot{\tilde{\gamma}},\mu\right)\,dt.

Using (
2.8
) and (
D.3
), we obtain

(D.4)

\displaystyle|\mathrm{I}|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4C_{1,H}}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{1}+\frac{1}{4n}}\left|4n(\phi_{n}(y)-y)\right|^{2}\,dt+\frac{1}{4C_{1,H}}\int_{t_{2}-\frac{1}{4n}}^{t_{2}}\left|4n\left(z-\phi_{n}(z)\right)\right|^{2}\,dt+\frac{K_{0}}{2n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\left(\frac{2}{C_{1,H}}+\frac{K_{0}}{2}\right),

and

(D.5)

|\mathrm{III}|\leq\frac{1}{2C_{1,H}}\int_{d}^{d+\frac{1}{n}}\left|\dot{\tilde{\gamma}}(t,w,y,z)\right|^{2}\,dt+\frac{K_{0}}{2n}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left(1+|y-z|^{2}\right)+\frac{K_{0}}{2n},

for some constant
C:=C(C_{1,H},C_{2,H},K_{0},t_{2}-t_{1})>0
. Write

m^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{n}(w),\mu;t_{1},t_{2},\phi_{n}(y),\phi_{n}(z)\right)=\tilde{{\rm II}}+\tilde{{\rm III}},

where

\tilde{{\rm II}}=\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\setminus[d,d+\frac{1}{n}]}L\left(\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{\varepsilon},\phi_{n}(w),\dot{\tilde{\gamma}},\mu\right)\,dt,\qquad\tilde{{\rm III}}=\int_{d}^{d+\frac{1}{n}}L\left(\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{\varepsilon},\phi_{n}(w),\dot{\tilde{\gamma}},\mu\right)\,dt.

Then by (
2.9
) and Höder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\left|{\rm II}-{\rm\tilde{II}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{L}\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\setminus[d,d+\frac{1}{n}]}\left(1+2\left|\dot{\tilde{\gamma}}(t,w,y,z)\right|\right)\left|\phi_{n}(w)-w\right|\,dt

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C_{L}(t_{2}-t_{1})}{n}+\frac{2C_{L}}{n}\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\left|\dot{\tilde{\gamma}}(t,w,y,z)\right|\,dt\leq\frac{C}{n^{\frac{5}{4}}}\left(1+|z-y|^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{C}{n},

and by (
2.9
) and (
D.3
),

\left|\tilde{{\rm III}}\right|\leq\frac{1}{C_{1,H}}\int_{d}^{d+\frac{1}{n}}\left|\dot{\tilde{\gamma}}(t,w,y,z)\right|^{2}\,dt+\frac{K_{0}}{n}\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left(1+|y-z|^{2}\right)+\frac{K_{0}}{n}.

Combining
(
D.3
), (
D.4
), and (
D.5
), for
n
large enough, we have

(D.6)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}L\left(\frac{\gamma_{n}}{\varepsilon},w,\dot{\gamma}_{n},\mu\right)\,dt-h^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{n}(w),\mu;t_{1},t_{2},\phi_{n}(y),\phi_{n}(z)\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}L\left(\frac{\gamma_{n}}{\varepsilon},w,\dot{\gamma}_{n},\mu\right)\,dt-\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}L\left(\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{\varepsilon},\phi_{n}(w),\dot{\tilde{\gamma}},\mu\right)\,dt,\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\mathrm{I}|+|\mathrm{III}|+\left|{\rm II}-\tilde{{\rm II}}\right|+\left|\tilde{{\rm III}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left(1+|y-z|^{2}\right).

By (
2.22
) and (
D.1
),

(D.7)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left|h^{\varepsilon}\left(\phi_{n}(w),\mu;t_{1},t_{2},\phi_{n}(y),\phi_{n}(z)\right)-h^{\varepsilon}\left(w,\mu;t_{1},t_{2},y,z\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Bigl[\Bigl(\left|w-\phi_{n}(w)\right|+\left|y-\phi_{n}(y)\right|+\lvert z-\phi_{n}(z)\rvert\Bigr)\Bigr.

\displaystyle\Bigl.\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Bigl(1+\lvert y-\phi_{n}(y)\rvert+\lvert z-\phi_{n}(z)\rvert+\lvert y-z\rvert^{2}+\lvert\phi_{n}(y)-\phi_{n}(z)\rvert^{2}\Bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3C}{n}\left(1+\frac{2}{n}+\frac{8}{n^{2}}+3\left|y-z\right|^{2}\right),

where
C=C(\varepsilon,C_{1,H},C_{2,H},C_{H},K_{0},t_{2}-t_{1})>0
. Combining (
D.6
) and (
D.7
), we obtain, for
n
sufficiently large,

\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}L\left(\frac{\gamma_{n}}{\varepsilon},w,\dot{\gamma}_{n},\mu\right)\,dt\leq h^{\varepsilon}\left(w,\mu;t_{1},t_{2},y,z\right)+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left(1+|y-z|^{2}\right),

where
\gamma_{n}:[t_{1},t_{2}]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
is jointly measurable.
∎

Remark D.2
.

The same conclusion remains valid under assumptions (A1’) and (A2), namely when
L
and
h^{\varepsilon}
are independent of
w,\mu
.
